Quick Learn 01 — GitHub & Git Basics 
Learning Text 
Git is a distributed version-control system used to track changes in files and collaborate on software. GitHub is a cloud 
platform built around Git that provides repositories, pull requests, issues, actions and collaboration features. 
Key Points 
●​
Git 
●​
GitHub 
●​
repository 
●​
commit 
●​
branch 
●​
merge 
15 MCQs 
Q1. Which tool tracks changes in a project’s files? 
●​
A. Git 
●​
B. GitHub 
●​
C. Docker 
●​
D. SQL 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Git is the version-control tool used to track changes. 
Q2. What is GitHub? 
●​
A. A hosted platform for Git repositories and collaboration 
●​
B. A programming language 
●​
C. A database engine 
●​
D. A computer network protocol 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: GitHub provides hosted repositories and collaboration features around Git. 
Q3. What does a Git commit represent? 
●​
A. A saved change in repository history 
●​
B. A remote server 
●​
C. A programming language 
●​
D. A network address 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: A commit records a set of changes in Git history. 
Q4. What is a branch used for? 
●​
A. A separate line of development 
●​
B. Encrypting a file 
●​
C. Running a database 
●​
D. Creating a network 
FEEDX Polytechnic Community • Quick Learn & Quiz Content Library 

--- PAGE 3 ---
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Branches let developers work on separate lines of development. 
Q5. What does git clone do? 
●​
A. Copies a repository to a local environment 
●​
B. Deletes a repository 
●​
C. Creates a password 
●​
D. Publishes a website 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Clone creates a local copy of a repository. 
Q6. What is a pull request? 
●​
A. A proposal to merge changes 
●​
B. A database query 
●​
C. A password reset 
●​
D. A cloud storage bucket 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: A pull request is used to propose and review changes before merging. 
Q7. What does git push do? 
●​
A. Sends local commits to a remote repository 
●​
B. Deletes commits 
●​
C. Creates an HTML page 
●​
D. Starts a firewall 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Push transfers local commits to a configured remote. 
Q8. What does git pull commonly do? 
●​
A. Fetches and integrates changes from a remote 
●​
B. Formats a disk 
●​
C. Creates a database 
●​
D. Encrypts a password 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Pull obtains remote changes and integrates them into the current branch. 
Q9. What is a repository? 
●​
A. A location containing a project's files and version history 
●​
B. A network cable 
●​
C. A browser 
●​
D. A password 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: A repository contains project files and version-control history. 
Q10. What is GitHub Actions commonly used for? 
●​
A. Automating workflows such as testing and deployment 
●​
B. Writing SQL queries 
●​
C. Drawing circuits 
●​
D. Managing IP addresses 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: GitHub Actions automates repository workflows. 
Q11. What is a fork on GitHub?

●​
A. A personal copy of another repository on GitHub 
●​
B. A local password 
●​
C. A network protocol 
●​
D. A compiler 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: A fork creates a separate copy of a repository under another account or organization. 
Q12. What does the staging area do in Git? 
●​
A. Prepares selected changes for a commit 
●​
B. Stores cloud backups 
●​
C. Runs a web server 
●​

B. Internet speed 
●​
C. Database tables 
●​
D. CPU temperature 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Git diff displays differences between file states. 
Q14. What is a Git tag often used for?

A. Naming a specific point such as a release 
●​
B. Creating a firewall 
●​
C. Storing passwords 
●​
D. Running a database 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Tags provide named references to particular commits, often releases. 
Q15. Why use version control? 
●​
A. To track changes and collaborate safely 
●​
B. To increase screen brightness 
●​
C. To replace an operating system 
●​
D. To compress videos 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Version control tracks changes and supports collaboration and recovery.

Quick Learn 02 — APIs & How They Work 
Learning Text 
An API is a defined way for software systems to communicate. Web APIs commonly use HTTP requests and responses. 
A client sends a request to an endpoint and the server returns data or a result, often using JSON. 
Key Points 
●​
API 
●​
endpoint 
FEEDX Polytechnic Community • Quick Learn & Quiz Content Library 

--- PAGE 5 ---
●​
HTTP 
●​
request 
●​
response 
●​
JSON 
15 MCQs 
Q1. What is an API? 
●​
A. An interface that lets software systems communicate 
●​
B. A type of database 
●​
C. A programming keyboard 
●​
D. A graphics card 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: An API defines how software components communicate. 
Q2. What is an API endpoint? 
●​
A. A specific address for an API resource or action 
●​
B. A password 
●​
C. A compiler 
●​
D. A storage device 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: An endpoint is a defined API URL/address for a resource or operation.

●​
C. DELETE

●​

●​
C. A mobile screen 
●​

Q14. What is a webhook?

B. A password hash 
●​
C. A CSS selector 
●​
D. A database index

Q15. Why is API documentation important?

●​
B. It increases CPU speed 
●​
C. It replaces testing 
●​
D. It stores photos 
Answer: A  |  Explanation: Documentation tells developers how to correctly use an API.

Quick Learn 03 — Cloud Computing 
Learning Text 
Cloud computing provides computing resources such as servers, storage, databases and networking over the internet. It 
allows organizations to scale resources and pay according to usage or service plans.

Quick Learn 04 — Cybersecurity Basics 
Learning Text 
Cybersecurity protects systems, networks, applications and data from unauthorized access, disruption and misuse. Core 
goals include confidentiality, integrity and availability, commonly called the CIA triad. 
Key Points

●​
threat

environments, networking and embedded systems. Users commonly interact with Linux through a shell.

Quick Learn 19 — TCP vs UDP 
Learning Text 
TCP provides reliable, ordered delivery using connections and acknowledgements. UDP is connectionless and does not 
provide the same built-in delivery guarantees, making it useful where low overhead is important.

Quick Learn 22 — JSON & XML

commonly used in modern web APIs, while XML uses tags and supports extensive document-oriented structures.

Quick Learn 24 — Encryption & Hashing 
Learning Text 
Encryption transforms data so it can be recovered with the appropriate key. Hashing produces a fixed-size digest and is 
designed to be one-way in normal use; password systems should use dedicated password-hashing algorithms.

Quick Learn 26 — Phishing & Social Engineering

Warning signs can include urgency, suspicious links, unexpected attachments and requests for secrets.

Quick Learn 47 — SQL vs NoSQL 
Learning Text 
Relational databases use structured tables and SQL. NoSQL is a broad category of databases using models such as 
document, key-value, wide-column or graph, often chosen for particular scalability or data-model needs.

CONTENT IMPLEMENTATION NOTES 
●​
Quick Learn = reading content + 15 MCQs. 
●​
Quiz = 20 MCQs + 35 FEEDX Points. 
●​
Each MCQ has four options, one correct answer and an explanation. 
●​
Content should be reviewed by FEEDX before publishing. 
●​
Admin should be able to edit questions, answers, explanations, points and publish status. 
●​
Questions should be randomized when the app runs a quiz if FEEDX enables question randomization. 
●​
Do not reveal correct answers before submission.
